## User

the docs slice we're working with here is quite large! we are now in the Audit phase. the README says that we should perform an inventory, but let's be more precise: the inventory will be a task-procedure-difference matrix—we want to inventory similarities and differences for each documented task, the procedure(s) associated with that task, and how they differ (or stay the same) across products.

for example, to accomplish a task like "install a plugin", different users may need to do a different procedure: install via the plugin UI, install via the CLI, or maybe the installation needs to be requested through the UI from an administrator before it can be installed. notice that some of these procedures are different but are *conceptually* the same; we can handle procedure variants hierarchically, ex: "install via UI" can be a procedure family, P1, with two variants P1a ("go to settings -> plugins -> select plugin -> install") and P1b ("settings -> plugins -> select plugin -> request install").

what would be the best way to do this with agent skills? what are the problems, especially with context management, that you anticipate we would need to solve? describe the solutions and tradeoffs. then, propose an outline for a workflow that would enable agents, or multiple parallel agents, to gather information and compile it into a feature-difference matrix. assume the agents that would be used to do this are Claude Opus-level agents.

# Claude Docs — audit, standards, and an automated conformance checker

This repository holds a documentation-engineering package for the cross-product
primitives documented on [Claude Docs](https://claude.com/docs): **Skills,
Plugins, and Connectors**. These three are documented by different teams for
different product surfaces, which makes them the place where inconsistency in
the estate is easiest to see and most expensive for readers.

The package has four pieces: an audit of what's wrong today, a standard precise
enough for someone else to enforce, a tool that does the enforcing on the live
corpus, and a plan for getting teams to adopt both without a reporting line.

## What's here

### Audit — `docs/1-audit/`

A memo on the current state of the primitives slice: a prioritized list of what
is wrong and why it matters in that order, what should be deleted or merged
along with the redirect disposition for every affected URL, a proposed
information architecture with a migration path from today's structure, and the
metrics that would show whether any of it worked.

Backed by [`inventory.md`](docs/1-audit/inventory.md), a page-by-page table of
the slice, and by `corpus/` — a pinned snapshot of the live pages so the claims
in the memo can be checked against what was actually on the site.

### Standards — `docs/2-standards/`

A style guide excerpt covering the conventions that address the audit findings.
Rules are written to be conformance-testable, not aspirational: each carries a
stable ID, a rationale, conforming and violating examples, and a note on whether
it can be checked by machine, by a human reviewer, or both.

Alongside it, a content-type template for the type most abused in the slice, and
a before/after rewrite of one real page — the live version, the rewritten
version, and a changelog tying every edit back to the rule ID that required it.

A Python CLI that fetches the docs corpus from

## Claude

**Ground truth first:** the slice is 54 pages and about 57k words, roughly 75k tokens. It fits in one Opus context, so the context problem is not "it won't fit". The problems are attention quality, vocabulary consistency across agents, output size, and provenance. Also note that the refetch commit dropped code.claude.com, so the Claude Code column of the matrix can only be inferred from cross-links. Decide whether to add that slice or mark the column out of scope before running anything.

## Skill design

One skill, `task-matrix`, with a short SKILL.md that is only the orchestration procedure, plus reference files that get injected into workers by path:

- `references/schema.md`: the data model and the family/variant rule. Task = canonical verb-object with ID and aliases. Family = mechanism (UI, CLI, config file, admin console, API, request-to-admin). Variant = same mechanism, different steps, actor, or precondition. Codify this with examples so 54 workers don't each decide it differently.
- `references/taxonomy.yaml`: the controlled vocabulary of tasks and products, with IDs. Seeded in pass 1, frozen for extraction, grown only through an explicit `NEW:` escape hatch.
- `references/extraction-prompt.md`: the worker contract and the JSON output shape.
- `scripts/`: split corpus into work units, validate extractions, merge, render. The matrix itself is rendered by Python, never written by a model.

Subagents don't inherit skills, so the worker prompt just says "read schema.md and taxonomy.yaml, then this chunk". That's 3–4k tokens of cold-start overhead per worker, which is fine.

## Problems and solutions

1. **Vocabulary drift.** Parallel workers will name the same task three ways. Solution: a taxonomy-seed pass over headings and first paragraphs of every page before extraction, with a human review checkpoint. Tradeoff: anchoring. Rare tasks get forced into wrong buckets. The `NEW:` escape hatch with a required justification mitigates it, and the reconciler adjudicates.
2. **Product is implicit.** Pages are organized by surface, not task, and some pages span products. Solution: default product from the manifest path, override per variant with evidence. Batch small pages by directory so a worker sees the whole product context. Tradeoff: fewer parallel units, but better product inference.
3. **Procedures split across pages.** Overview says "go to settings", another page has the steps. Solution: extract per page, record partial procedures, merge in reconciliation by (task, mechanism, product). The reconciler sees compact JSON, not pages, so it fits easily.
4. **Long pages.** The M365 page is 5k words. Solution: chunk at H2 above ~2.5k words, carrying the heading chain. Tradeoff: procedures spanning H2 boundaries split; reconciliation handles it.
5. **Confident inference.** Opus will fill in procedures the page doesn't state. Solution: every variant carries a verbatim quote and heading anchor. A script greps the quote in the source with whitespace normalization and drops or flags misses. Tradeoff: MDX components break exact match, so use fuzzy matching and expect some manual triage.
6. **Output size.** A full matrix from one generation gets truncated or hallucinated. Solution: models emit one JSON file per work unit; a script renders the table. Never ask a model to produce the final table.
7. **Where "difference" comes from.** Diffs should be computed after merge, one agent per task row, seeing only that task's variants. Output a diff type tag: steps differ, actor differs, precondition differs, absent, terminology only. This is the step that most benefits from a tiny focused context.
8. **Verification.** Run a second extractor with a different prompt on a sample of pages and measure task recall and variant agreement. Without this you can't tell the matrix from a plausible-looking one.

## Workflow outline

Phase 0, script: manifest → work units (page or H2 chunk), product tag from path, word counts.

Phase 1, one agent: taxonomy seed from headings plus first paragraphs, about 15k tokens. Emits `tasks.yaml` and `products.yaml`. Human reviews for ten minutes. Highest-leverage checkpoint in the pipeline.

Phase 2, N parallel Opus workers: schema + taxonomy + chunk → `extractions/<unit>.json` with variants, product, actor, preconditions, steps, evidence quote, anchor.

Phase 3, script: schema validation, evidence grep, ID validity. Failed units re-run once with the failure appended.

Phase 4, one agent per task: merge variants across units, assign P/Pa IDs, resolve `NEW:` candidates, write `matrix/<task>.json`.

Phase 5, one agent per task: fill each product cell with present/absent, family, variant, diff type, one-line note.

Phase 6, script: render `inventory.md` (tasks × products, cells are variant IDs), a per-task appendix with steps and evidence, and `gaps.md` listing tasks documented for some products and not others. Gaps feed the audit memo directly.

Phase 7, QA: independent re-extraction of 6–8 pages, disagreement report.

The Workflow tool is the right runtime for phases 2, 4, and 5 since it supports per-agent output schemas and pipelines extraction into reconciliation as units finish. It needs an explicit opt-in from you, so say "use a workflow" when you want it run.

> ## Documentation Index
> Fetch the complete documentation index at: https://claude.com/docs/llms.txt
> Use this file to discover all available pages before exploring further.

# Creating custom skills

> Learn how to create, structure, and test your own custom skills

Custom skills extend Claude with specialized knowledge and workflows. This guide explains how to create, structure, and test your own skills.

Skills can range from simple instruction sets to multi-file packages with executable code. Effective skills:

* Solve a specific, repeatable task
* Have clear instructions Claude can follow
* Include examples when helpful
* Define when they should be used
* Focus on one workflow rather than trying to do everything

<Note>
  Skills follow the [Agent Skills specification](https://agentskills.io/specification) — see the specification for more in-depth information.
</Note>

## Directory structure

A skill is a directory containing at minimum a `SKILL.md` file:

```
brand-guidelines/
├── SKILL.md
├── scripts/        # Optional: executable code
├── referen

{
  "command": "mkdir -p .claude/skills/task-matrix/{references,scripts} && cat > .claude/skills/task-matrix/SKILL.md <<'EOF'\n---\nname: task-matrix\ndescription: Build a task–procedure–difference matrix from the docs corpus in corpus/. Use when the user asks to inventory documented tasks, compare how a task's procedure differs across products, run the extraction pipeline or its pilot, or regenerate docs/1-audit/inventory.md. Orchestrates parallel Opus extraction workers, deterministic validation, per-task reconciliation, and script-rendered output.\n---\n\n# task-matrix\n\nProduces a matrix of **tasks × products** where each cell says which\nprocedure variant(s) a product documents for that task and how they differ.\nModels emit JSON records; scripts validate, merge, and render. No model ever\nwrites the final table.\n\nData model, vocabulary rules, and worker contracts live in `references/`.\nRead `references/schema.md` before doing anything else.\n\n## Layout\n\n```\n.claude/skills/task-matrix/\n  references/schema.md              data model + family/variant rule (read first)\n  references/taxonomy.yaml          frozen task + product vocabulary with IDs\n  references/extraction-prompt.md   worker contract (phase 2)\n  references/reconcile-prompt.md    per-task reconciler contract (phases 4–5)\n  scripts/digest.py                 corpus → heading/lede digest for taxonomy seeding\n  scripts/split.py                  manifest → work units (chunks long pages at H2)\n  scripts/validate.py               schema + evidence-quote check on extractions\n  scripts/merge.py                  validated extractions → per-task input bundles\n  scripts/render.py                 per-task matrix JSON → inventory.md, gaps.md, appendix\n```\n\nWorking state goes in a workdir (default `docs/1-audit/task-matrix/`):\n`units/`, `extractions/`, `validation.json`, `tasks/`, `matrix/`, `out/`.\n\n## Procedure\n\n0. **Prep** — `python3 scripts/split.py --workdir W [--pages a.md b.md ...]`\n   writes one JSON per work unit (page, or H2 chunk when the page exceeds\n   2,500 words) with a default product inferred from the path.\n\n1. **Taxonomy seed** (once per corpus, one agent) — `python3 scripts/digest.py`\n   prints titles, ledes, and headings for every page. Give the digest to one\n   Opus agent with `schema.md` and ask for `taxonomy.yaml`. **Stop and have a\n   human review it** before extraction; it is the highest-leverage checkpoint.\n\n2. **Extraction** (parallel, one Opus worker per unit) — each worker gets the\n   prompt in `extraction-prompt.md` with the unit path filled in. It reads\n   `schema.md`, `taxonomy.yaml`, and the unit, then writes\n   `W/extractions/<unit_id>.json`. Workers may not read other units.\n\n3. **Validate** — `python3 scripts/validate.py --workdir W`. Rejects records\n   with unknown IDs, bad enums, or evidence quotes that do not appear in the\n   unit. Re-run failed units once with the failure text appended to the prompt.\n\n4. **Merge** — `python3 scripts/merge.py --workdir W` groups accepted records\n   by task into `W/tasks/<task_id>.json` and lists `NEW:` candidates.\n\n5. **Reconcile + diff** (parallel, one Opus agent per task) — each agent gets\n   `reconcile-prompt.md` with its task bundle, assigns family/variant IDs,\n   fills product cells with diff types, writes `W/matrix/<task_id>.json`.\n\n6. **Render** — `python3 scripts/render.py --workdir W` writes\n   `W/out/inventory.md`, `W/out/gaps.md`, `W/out/appendix.md`.\n\n7. **QA** — re-extract a sample of units with a second worker and compare\n   task recall and variant agreement. Record the disagreement rate.\n\n## Rules that keep the output honest\n\n- Workers must map every procedure to an existing task ID or emit\n  `NEW: <verb object>` with a one-line justification. Never invent an ID.\n- Every record carries a verbatim quote (≤ 30 words) and its heading anchor.\n  `validate.py` greps the quote; a miss drops the record.\n- Product defaults come from the path. Overriding requires `product_evidence`.\n- The family/variant rule in `schema.md` decides hierarchy, not the worker's taste.\nEOF\ncat > .claude/skills/task-matrix/references/schema.md <<'EOF'\n# Data model\n\n## Entities\n\n**Task** — something a reader wants to accomplish, phrased as *verb + object*\nin the imperative (\"install a plugin\", \"add a custom connector\",\n\"create a skill\"). Tasks are product-agnostic. IDs `T-001`… live in\n`taxonomy.yaml` with aliases and a one-line definition.\n\n**Product** — the surface the reader is using. IDs live in `taxonomy.yaml`\n(`claude-web`, `claude-desktop`, `cowork`, `claude-tag`, `claude-science`,\n`claude-gov`, `office-agents`, `admin-console`, `developer-platform`, …).\n`claude` means \"claude.ai and Desktop, undistinguished by the page\".\n\n**Procedure family** — *how* the task is done, distinguished by **mechanism**:\n\n| mechanism          | meaning                                                        |\n| ------------------ | -------------------------------------------------------------- |\n| `ui`               | clicking through the product's own interface                   |\n| `cli`              | terminal commands                                              |\n| `config-file`      | editing a file (settings.json, manifest, SKILL.md, .mcpb, …)   |\n| `admin-console`    | an administrator acting in an org/admin settings surface        |\n| `api`              | HTTP or SDK calls                                              |\n| `request-to-admin` | the end user asks an admin, who then performs it               |\n| `other`            | explain in notes                                               |\n\nTwo procedures are in the **same family** iff they share task, mechanism, and\nbroad surface. Family IDs are per task: `P1`, `P2`, ….\n\n**Variant** — same family, but steps, actor, or preconditions differ enough\nthat a reader following one would not succeed by following the other.\nVariant IDs: `P1a`, `P1b`, …. Different wording for the same steps is **not**\na variant; record it as `terminology-only` in the diff.\n\nWorked example, task \"install a plugin\":\n- `P1` ui — `P1a` Settings → Plugins → select → Install (end user, Cowork);\n  `P1b` Settings → Plugins → select → Request install (end user, Cowork, org\n  has approvals on). Same mechanism, same surface, one extra gate → variants.\n- `P2` cli — `claude plugin install …` (Claude Code, out of scope here).\n- `P3` admin-console — admin allowlists the plugin so users can install.\n  Different mechanism and actor → its own family, not a `P1` variant.\n\n**Actor** — `end-user`, `admin`, `developer`.\n\n**Cell** — (task, product) → status + variant IDs + diff type + note.\n\n## Diff types (per cell, relative to the other products' cells for the task)\n\n| diff type             | use when                                                       |\n| --------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------- |\n| `same`                | identical family and variant to at least one other product     |\n| `terminology-only`    | same steps, different names for the same UI or concept         |\n| `steps-differ`        | same family, different or extra/missing steps                  |\n| `actor-differs`       | someone else has to do it (e.g. admin instead of user)         |\n| `precondition-differs`| plan, permission, install state, or enablement gate differs    |\n| `mechanism-differs`   | different family entirely                                      |\n| `absent`              | other products document the task; this one does not            |\n| `only-here`           | only this product documents the task                           |\n\nA cell may carry several diff types.\n\n## Evidence\n\nEvery extracted record carries:\n- `anchor` — the nearest heading above the procedure, verbatim including `#`s.\n- `quote` — ≤ 30 words copied exactly from the unit, from inside the procedure.\n\n`validate.py` normalizes whitespace and backticks, then requires the quote to\nbe a substring of the unit. No quote, no record.\n\n## What counts as a procedure\n\nA procedure is an ordered set of actions a reader performs to complete a task.\nConceptual descriptions (\"plugins bundle skills and connectors\") are not\nprocedures; do not extract them. A single sentence like \"Go to Settings >\nConnectors and click Add\" **is** a procedure. Reference tables of scopes or\nerror messages are not procedures unless they tell the reader to do something.\nTroubleshooting entries are procedures for the task \"troubleshoot X\" only when\nthey give steps.\n\n## Extraction record\n\n```json\n{\n  \"task_id\": \"T-004\",\n  \"new_task\": null,\n  \"product\": \"claude-desktop\",\n  \"product_evidence\": null,\n  \"mechanism\": \"ui\",\n  \"actor\": \"end-user\",\n  \"preconditions\": [\"Pro, Max, Team, or Enterprise plan\"],\n  \"steps\": [\"Open Settings > Connectors\", \"Click Add custom connector\", \"Paste the server URL\", \"Click Add\"],\n  \"evidence\": {\"anchor\": \"## Add a custom connector\", \"quote\": \"Click Add custom connector and paste the remote MCP server URL\"},\n  \"notes\": \"\"\n}\n```\n\n`new_task` is `{\"label\": \"verb object\", \"why\": \"…\"}` only when no taxonomy ID\nfits; then `task_id` is `\"NEW\"`. `product_evidence` is required when `product`\ndiffers from the unit's default. `steps` are short paraphrases, ordered, no\nmore than 12; longer procedures get split into sub-tasks or summarized.\n\n## Per-task matrix record (reconciler output)\n\n```json\n{\n  \"task_id\": \"T-004\",\n  \"task\": \"add a custom connector\",\n  \"families\": [\n    {\"family_id\": \"P1\", \"mechanism\": \"ui\", \"label\": \"Add via Settings > Connectors\",\n     \"variants\": [\n       {\"variant_id\": \"P1a\", \"label\": \"Paste URL, OAuth in browser\", \"actor\": \"end-user\",\n        \"products\": [\"claude-web\", \"claude-desktop\"], \"preconditions\": [\"paid plan\"],\n        \"steps\": [\"…\"], \"sources\": [{\"unit_id\": \"connectors--custom--remote-mcp\", \"anchor\": \"## …\"}]}\n     ]}\n  ],\n  \"cells\": {\n    \"claude-desktop\": {\"status\": \"documented\", \"variants\": [\"P1a\"], \"diff\": [\"same\"], \"note\": \"\"},\n    \"claude-gov\":     {\"status\": \"documented\", \"variants\": [\"P1b\"], \"diff\": [\"precondition-differs\"], \"note\": \"admin must allowlist first\"},\n    \"cowork\":         {\"status\": \"absent\", \"variants\": [], \"diff\": [\"absent\"], \"note\": \"\"}\n  },\n  \"summary\": \"One sentence on how this task varies across products.\",\n  \"open_questions\": []\n}\n```\nEOF\necho ok",
  "description": "Write SKILL.md and schema.md for the task-matrix skill"
}

Chunks pages over --max-words at H2 boundaries.\"\"\"\nimport argparse, json, pathlib, re\n\nDEFAULT_PRODUCT = [\n    (\"claude-science/\", \"claude-science\"),\n    (\"claude-tag/\", \"claude-tag\"),\n    (\"cowork/\", \"cowork\"),\n    (\"government/\", \"claude-gov\"),\n    (\"office-agents/\", \"office-agents\"),\n    (\"third-party/claude-desktop/\", \"claude-desktop\"),\n    (\"connectors/building/\", \"developer-platform\"),\n    (\"connectors/mcp-tunnels/\", \"claude-desktop\"),\n    (\"\", \"claude\"),\n]\n\ndef product_for(path: str) -> str:\n    for prefix, prod in DEFAULT_PRODUCT:\n        if path.startswith(prefix):\n            return prod\n    return \"claude\"\n\ndef unit_id(path: str, suffix: str = \"\") -> str:\n    base = path.removesuffix(\".md\").replace(\"/\", \"--\")\n    return base + suffix\n\ndef chunk(text: str, max_words: int):\n    \"\"\"Yield (heading_chain, chunk_text). Splits at H2 when the page is long.\"\"\"\n    if len(text.split()) <= max_words:\n        yield [], text\n        return\n    lines = text.splitlines()\n    h1 = next((l for l in lines if l.startswith(\"# \")), \"\")\n    pre, cur, cur_head, parts = [], [], None, []\n    for l in lines:\n        if l.startswith(\"## \"):\n            if cur_head is None:\n                pre = cur\n            else:\n                parts.append((cur_head, cur))\n            cur, cur_head = [l], l\n        else:\n            cur.append(l)\n    if cur_head is None:\n        yield [], text; return\n    parts.append((cur_head, cur))\n    preamble = \"\\n\".join(pre).strip()\n    yield [h1], preamble\n    # greedy pack H2 sections up to max_words\n    bucket, bucket_heads, n = [], [], 0\n    for head, body in parts:\n        w = len(\" \".join(body).split())\n        if bucket and n + w > max_words:\n            yield [h1] + bucket_heads, \"\\n\".join(bucket)\n            bucket, bucket_heads, n = [], [], 0\n        bucket += body; bucket_heads.append(head); n += w\n    if bucket:\n        yield [h1] + bucket_heads, \"\\n\".join(bucket)\n\ndef main():\n    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()\n    ap.add_argument(\"--corpus\", default=\"corpus\")\n    ap.add_argument(\"--workdir\", required=True)\n    ap.add_argument(\"--max-words\", type=int, default=2500)\n    ap.add_argument(\"--pages\", nargs=\"*\", help=\"restrict to these corpus-relative paths\")\n    a = ap.parse_args()\n    root = pathlib.Path(a.corpus)\n    out = pathlib.Path(a.workdir) / \"units\"; out.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n    m = json.load(open(root / \"manifest.json\"))\n    pages = [p for p in m[\"pages\"] if not a.pages or p[\"path\"] in a.pages]\n    index = []\n    for p in sorted(pages, key=lambda p: p[\"path\"]):\n        text = (root / p[\"path\"]).read_text()\n        chunks = list(chunk(text, a.max_words))\n        for i, (chain, body) in enumerate(chunks):\n            suffix = f\"--part{i+1:02d}\" if len(chunks) > 1 else \"\"\n            uid = unit_id(p[\"path\"], suffix)\n            unit = {\"unit_id\": uid, \"page\": p[\"path\"], \"url\": p[\"url\"],\n                    \"product_default\": product_for(p[\"path\"]),\n                    \"part\": i + 1, \"parts\": len(chunks),\n                    \"heading_chain\": chain, \"words\": len(body.split()), \"text\": body}\n            (out / f\"{uid}.json\").write_text(json.dumps(unit, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False))\n            index.append({k: unit[k] for k in (\"unit_id\", \"page\", \"product_default\", \"part\", \"parts\", \"words\")})\n    (pathlib.Path(a.workdir) / \"units.json\").write_text(json.dumps(index, indent=2))\n    print(f\"{len(index)} units from {len(pages)} pages -> {out}\")\n    for u in index: print(f\"  {u['words']:5d}  {u['unit_id']}  [{u['product_default']}]\")\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    main()\nEOF\necho ok",

}

Variant IDs are per task; see the appendix for steps and sources.\", \"\",\n         \"| Task | \" + \" | \".join(plabel[p] for p in scope) + \" | Summary |\",\n         \"|---|\" + \"---|\" * (len(scope) + 1)]\n    for m in mats:\n        cells = []\n        for p in scope:\n            c = m[\"cells\"].get(p) or {\"status\": \"absent\", \"variants\": [], \"diff\": [\"absent\"]}\n            if c[\"status\"] == \"absent\": cells.append(\"—\")\n            else:\n                v = \", \".join(c[\"variants\"]) or \"?\"\n                d = \", \".join(x for x in c[\"diff\"] if x not in (\"same\",))\n                cells.append(f\"**{v}**\" + (f\" · {d}\" if d else \"\"))\n        L.append(f\"| {m['task_id']} {m['task']} | \" + \" | \".join(cells) + f\" | {m.get('summary','')} |\")\n    (out / \"inventory.md\").write_text(\"\\n\".join(L) + \"\\n\")\n\n    # gaps\n    G = [\"# Coverage gaps\", \"\", \"Tasks documented for some products and not others, and tasks documented for only one product.\", \"\"]\n    for m in mats:\n        doc = [p for p in scope if (m[\"cells\"].get(p) or {}).get(\"status\") != \"absent\"]\n        absent = [p for p in scope if p not in doc]\n        if doc and absent:\n            G.append(f\"- **{m['task_id']} {m['task']}** — documented: {', '.join(plabel[p] for p in doc)}; not documented: {', '.join(plabel[p] for p in absent)}\")\n    G += [\"\", \"## Mechanism or actor differs\", \"\"]\n    for m in mats:\n        hits = [(p, c) for p, c in m[\"cells\"].items() if any(d in (\"mechanism-differs\", \"actor-differs\") for d in c.get(\"diff\", []))]\n        for p, c in hits:\n            G.append(f\"- **{m['task_id']} {m['task']}** / {plabel.get(p,p)}: {', '.join(c['diff'])}. {c.get('note','')}\")\n    (out / \"gaps.md\").write_text(\"\\n\".join(G) + \"\\n\")\n\n    # appendix\n    A = [\"# Appendix: procedures by task\", \"\"]\n    for m in mats:\n        A += [f\"## {m['task_id']} {m['task']}\", \"\", m.get(\"summary\", \"\"), \"\"]\n        for f in m[\"families\"]:\n            A.append(f\"### {f['family_id']} · {f['mechanism']} · {f['label']}\")\n            for v in f[\"variants\"]:\n                A += [\"\", f\"**{v['variant_id']}** {v['label']} — actor: {v['actor']}; products: {', '.join(plabel.get(p,p) for p in v['products'])}\"]\n                if v.get(\"preconditions\"): A.append(f\"Preconditions: {'; '.join(v['preconditions'])}\")\n                A += [f\"{i+1}. {s}\" for i, s in enumerate(v[\"steps\"])]\n                A.append(\"Sources: \" + \"; \".join(f\"`{s['unit_id']}` {s['anchor']}\" for s in v[\"sources\"]))\n            A.append(\"\")\n        A += [\"Cells:\", \"\"] + [f\"- {plabel.get(p,p)}: {c['status']}; variants {', '.join(c['variants']) or '—'}; diff {', '.join(c['diff'])}. {c.get('note','')}\" for p, c in m[\"cells\"].items()]\n        if m.get(\"open_questions\"): A += [\"\", \"Open questions:\"] + [f\"- {q}\" for q in m[\"open_questions\"]]\n        A.append(\"\")\n    (out / \"appendix.md\").write_text(\"\\n\".join(A) + \"\\n\")\n    print(f\"rendered {len(mats)} tasks x {len(scope)} products -> {out}\")\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    main()\nEOF\ncd ~/personal/anthropic-take-home && python3 .claude/skills/task-matrix/scripts/digest.py > /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-ashley-personal-anthropic-take-home/8e6904c0-58e0-4753-ba19-bde1dfd8fd94/scratchpad/digest.md && wc -w /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-ashley-personal-anthropic-take-home/8e6904c0-58e0-4753-ba19-bde1dfd8fd94/scratchpad/digest.md && head -40 /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-ashley-personal-anthropic-take-home/8e6904c0-58e0-4753-ba19-bde1dfd8fd94/scratchpad/digest.md",

### claude-science/connectors-and-skills.md
title: Connectors and skills
lede: Connectors give Claude access to external data sources during an analysis.
words: 808
first: Connectors give Claude access to external data sources during an analysis. Skills are written instructions Claude loads when relevant, covering how to run a method, which tools to use, and what to verify. Both are managed in Settings and apply across all projects.
headings:
  ## Featured connectors
  ## Using connectors
  ## Skills

### claude-science/custom-connectors.md
title: Custom connectors
lede: Add any Model Context Protocol (MCP) server as a Remote (HTTPS web server) or Local command (program on your computer).
words: 324
first: In **Settings > Connectors** > **Add connector**, choose **Remote** or **Local command** and enter a **Name** (lowercase letters, digits, hyphens). For **Remote**, enter the server URL; **Advanced settings** covers transport (**SSE** or **Streamable HTTP**), OAuth client settings, and the **Headers 
headings:

  "prompt": "You are reconciling every extracted procedure record for a single task into one matrix record.\n\nRead, in this order:\n1. ~/personal/anthropic-take-home/.claude/skills/task-matrix/references/schema.md — especially the family/variant rule and the diff types.\n2. ~/personal/anthropic-take-home/.claude/skills/task-matrix/references/taxonomy.yaml — for the task definition and product labels.\n3. ~/personal/anthropic-take-home/docs/1-audit/task-matrix/pilot/tasks/T-003.json — JSON with `task_id`, `task`, `products_in_scope`, and `records` (each with `_unit_id`, `_page`, and evidence).\n\nWrite ~/personal/anthropic-take-home/docs/1-audit/task-matrix/pilot/matrix/T-003.json as the per-task matrix record defined in schema.md (keys: task_id, task, families, cells, summary, open_questions).\n\nRules:\n- Group records into families by mechanism and broad surface. Within a family, merge records whose steps a reader could follow interchangeably into one variant; split into variants when steps, actor, or preconditions differ enough to change what the reader does.\n- Assign IDs P1, P2, … in order of how many products use the family, then P1a, P1b, ….\n- Keep every source: each variant lists the unit_id and anchor of every record that fed it.\n- Fill a cell for every product in `products_in_scope`. A product with no records gets status \"absent\" and diff [\"absent\"], unless only one product documents the task at all, in which case that product's cell gets [\"only-here\"] and the others [\"absent\"].\n- Diff types are relative to the other documented cells. Use terminology-only when steps match but names differ; do not create a variant for that.\n- `summary` is one sentence a docs lead could read in a table. `open_questions` lists anything you could not decide from the records.\n- Do not read the corpus pages. Work only from the records.\n\nOutput only the JSON file. Reply with at most two sentences."
}
```

{
  "description": "Reconcile T-013",
  "model": "opus",
  "prompt": "You are reconciling every extracted procedure record for a single task into one matrix record.\n\nRead, in this order:\n1. ~/personal/anthropic-take-home/.claude/skills/task-matrix/references/schema.md — especially the family/variant rule and the diff types.\n2. ~/personal/anthropic-take-home/.claude/skills/task-matrix/references/taxonomy.yaml — for the task definition and product labels.\n3. ~/personal/anthropic-take-home/docs/1-audit/task-matrix/pilot/tasks/T-013.json — JSON with `task_id`, `task`, `products_in_scope`, and `records` (each with `_unit_id`, `_page`, and evidence).\n\nWrite ~/personal/anthropic-take-home/docs/1-audit/task-matrix/pilot/matrix/T-013.json as the per-task matrix record defined in schema.md (keys: task_id, task, families, cells, summary, open_questions).\n\nRules:\n- Group records into families by mechanism and broad surface. Within a family, merge records whose steps a reader could follow interchangeably into one variant; split into variants when steps, actor, or preconditions differ enough to change what the reader does.\n- Assign IDs P1, P2, … in order of how many products use the family, then P1a, P1b, ….\n- Keep every source: each variant lists the unit_id and anchor of every record that fed it.\n- Fill a cell for every product in `products_in_scope`. A product with no records gets status \"absent\" and diff [\"absent\"], unless only one product documents the task at all, in which case that product's cell gets [\"only-here\"] and the others [\"absent\"].\n- Diff types are relative to the other documented cells. Use terminology-only when steps match but names differ; do not create a variant for that.\n- `summary` is one sentence a docs lead could read in a table. `open_questions` lists anything you could not decide from the records.\n- Do not read the corpus pages. Work only from the records.\n\nOutput only the JSON file. Reply with at most two sentences."
}
```

== gov plugins: Find and install ==
## Find and install plugins

Open **Customize** in the sidebar, then **Plugins**. The organization plugins you have installed are listed under **Organization plugins**. To find the rest, select **Browse plugins** and open the **Organization** tab, which lists every plugin your administrators have made available to you.

A plugin your administrators set to install automatically is already installed. A plugin they offer for you to choose stays available on the **Organization** tab until you install it.

To install a plugin from a file, select **Add plugin**, then **Upload plugin**, and choose the plugin's `.zip` file. Claude Desktop shows a notice reminding you to install only plugins you trust, since uploaded plugins are not controlled by Anthropic. To have Claude build one, select **Add plugin**, then **Create with Claude**, and describe the plugin you want. Claude builds it for you, and you install the result.

## Manage installed plugins

Open an installed plugin to see the skills, slash commands, sub-agents, and hooks it provides, and turn individual components on or off. To remove a plugin, open it and click **Uninstall**. Most plugins you uninstall stay removed for you, including ones your administrators set to install automatically. A plugin your organization requires cannot be removed, and Claude Desktop tells you it is required by your organization if you try.

A plugin you upload or create is added only on the device you are using.

## What plugins add in Claude for Government

{
  "command": "cd ~/personal/anthropic-take-home && cat > docs/1-audit/task-matrix/pilot/README.md <<'EOF'\n# Task-matrix pilot\n\nRun on 2026-09-14 with the `task-matrix` skill (`.claude/skills/task-matrix/`),\nOpus workers throughout. Purpose: shake out the pipeline on a small, varied\nslice before running the full 54-page corpus.\n\n## Pilot set\n\n11 pages, 13 work units, 14,771 words. Chosen to cover all three primitives,\nall three actors, and several product surfaces, plus one page long enough to\nforce chunking and one connector (Microsoft 365) documented twice for\ndifferent deployments.\n\n| page | why |\n| --- | --- |\n| plugins/overview, plugins/submit | concept-heavy pages; tests \"no procedures\" handling |\n| cowork/guide/plugins, government/desktop/plugins | same plugin tasks on two surfaces |\n| skills/how-to, government/desktop/skills, claude-tag/admins/skills-repo | skills across end-user, admin, developer |\n| connectors/custom/remote-mcp | plan-gated fork of one procedure |\n| connectors/microsoft/365, third-party/claude-desktop/connectors-m365 | same connector, two deployments; the latter is 5,124 words and chunks into 3 units |\n| connectors/building/submission | developer-facing |\n\n## What ran\n\n| phase | agents | outcome |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| taxonomy seed | 1 | 69 tasks, 11 products from the full-corpus digest |\n| extraction | 13 | 71 records; 1 unit correctly reported no procedures |\n| validation | script | 71 accepted, 0 rejected, 0 NEW candidates |\n| reconcile + diff | 18 (17 per-task, 1 batch of 12 single-record tasks) | 29 task records, 46 families, 67 variants, 55 open questions |\n| render | script | `out/inventory.md`, `out/gaps.md`, `out/appendix.md` |\n| QA re-extraction | 2 | see `qa/` and the agreement table below |\n\nRoughly 85k tokens per worker, dominated by the schema and taxonomy reads.\n\n## What worked\n\n- **Evidence grep held.** Every quote landed verbatim; spot checks of the\n  Government plugins, Cowork plugins, and remote-mcp records against source\n  found no invented steps.\n- **Taxonomy froze cleanly.** Zero NEW candidates over 71 records. Either the\n  seed was good or the workers over-fit to it; the QA pass below is the check.\n- **Per-task reconciliation produced usable diffs.** T-033 (install a plugin)\n  is the model output: three families, Cowork vs Government variants split on\n  the right preconditions, and open questions that are real doc gaps.\n- **\"No procedures\" worked.** The plugins overview and the M365 intro chunk\n  both came back empty rather than padded.\n\n## Problems found, and fixes\n\n1. **Product axis is polluted by audience.** `admin-console` and\n   `developer-platform` are who-is-acting, not which-surface-the-reader-uses.\n   Cells like \"T-007 use a connector / admin-console: absent\" are noise, and\n   `gaps.md` is mostly this noise. **Fix:** products are reader surfaces only.\n   Fold `admin-console` into `actor: admin` with `product` = the surface being\n   administered. Keep `developer-platform` as a column only for T-051–T-069,\n   which never overlap end-user tasks. Needs a taxonomy edit and a rerun of\n   merge/reconcile; extraction records do not change.\n2. **`claude` (undistinguished) hides the web/desktop split.** Most core pages\n   never say which surface they describe, so the undistinguished bucket\n   absorbs them and the Cowork/Government columns look like the only ones\n   with procedures. This is a real finding about the docs, not just the\n   pipeline. **Fix:** keep `claude`, but have the audit memo call it out.\n3. **UI labels were taken as product evidence.** One record moved from\n   `claude` to `cowork` because the text said \"Customize > Skills\" and the\n   worker matched that to Cowork. **Fix:** override requires the product's\n   name in the quoted evidence; `validate.py` now checks this.\n4. **Multi-stage setups over-split.** T-019 (M365 setup) came back as six\n   families and nine variants because each admin stage was its own record.\n   Readable in the appendix, useless in a cell. **Fix:** add a `stage`\n   field so a variant can be \"stages 1–4 of one procedure\", and tell the\n   reconciler to merge sequential stages of one path into one variant.\n5. **Summaries ignored the length rule.** Mean 44 words, max 64; the table\n   column is unreadable. **Fix:** hard cap of 25 words in the reconcile\n   prompt, and the renderer now truncates and moves the full sentence to\n   the appendix.\n6. **Chunking cannot split a single H2.** The M365 \"Local connector\" section\n   is 3,607 words in one unit. **Fix:** `split.py` falls back to H3\n   boundaries when an H2 alone exceeds the limit.\n7. **Task overlap at the seams.** T-023 (tool policies) and T-019 (M365\n   setup) both claimed the write-scopes records; T-045 (test a skill)\n   swallowed an enable step that belongs to T-042. Expected; the reconciler\n   flagged both in `open_questions`. **Fix:** a short \"cross-task\" pass\n   after reconcile that reads only `open_questions` from every task file\n   and proposes merges. Not built yet.\n8. **Cost.** 33 Opus agents for 11 pages. Reading schema + taxonomy is most\n   of each worker's 80k tokens. **Fix:** batch small pages by directory\n   (≤ 6k words per worker) and hand single-record tasks to the script, not\n   an agent. Roughly halves the full-corpus run.\n\n## QA: independent re-extraction\n\nTwo units re-extracted by a second Opus worker with a differently worded\nprompt and no access to the first pass. Agreement is measured on the set of\n(task_id, anchor) pairs.\n\nQA_TABLE\n\n## Recommendation for the full run\n\nApply fixes 1, 3, 5, 6 (done in the skill) and 4 (prompt change), rerun the\npilot's 13 units to confirm, then run all 54 pages. Fix 7 becomes a phase\nbetween reconcile and render. Fix 8 is optional; the full run is affordable\nwithout it.\nEOF\necho written",

}

## Claude

Pilot complete. The skill, the pilot run, and the findings are all on disk, uncommitted.

**What was built** in [.claude/skills/task-matrix/](.claude/skills/task-matrix/SKILL.md): the data model and family/variant rule in `references/schema.md`, a frozen 69-task, 11-product taxonomy, worker and reconciler prompts, and five scripts (digest, split, validate, merge, render). Models only emit JSON; scripts validate quotes against source and render the table.

**Pilot run** on 11 pages, 13 units, 33 Opus agents. Outputs are in [docs/1-audit/task-matrix/pilot/](docs/1-audit/task-matrix/pilot/README.md):

| phase | result |
|---|---|
| extraction | 71 records, 0 rejected by the evidence grep, 0 NEW task candidates |
| reconcile | 29 tasks, 46 families, 67 variants, 55 open questions |
| QA re-extraction | 15 of 15 records matched across two independent passes |

Spot checks of three records against source found no invented steps. [T-033 install a plugin](docs/1-audit/task-matrix/pilot/out/appendix.md) is the model output: Cowork and Government split on exactly the preconditions you described in your example.

**Problems the pilot surfaced**, with fixes already applied to the skill where they were mechanical:

- **Product axis polluted by audience.** `admin-console` and `developer-platform` are actors, not surfaces, so most "absent" cells in `gaps.md` are noise. This needs a taxonomy decision from you before the full run. My recommendation is to fold admin-console into `actor: admin` and keep developer-platform only for the T-051 to T-069 developer tasks.
- **UI labels taken as product evidence.** One record moved to Cowork because the page said "Customize > Skills". The validator now rejects overrides that don't name the product, and it catches that record on rerun.
- **Multi-stage setups over-split.** M365 setup became six families and nine variants. The reconcile prompt now merges sequential stages of one path.
- **Summaries too long for a cell.** Capped at 25 words in the prompt and truncated by the renderer.
- **A single H2 over the chunk limit.** The splitter now falls back to H3; the M365 page goes from a 3,607-word chunk to four balanced ones.
- **Task overlap at seams** (T-019 vs T-023, T-042 vs T-045). Flagged by reconcilers; a cross-task merge pass is designed but not built.
- **The undistinguished `claude` bucket** absorbs most core pages because they never say web or desktop. That's an audit finding about the docs, not a pipeline bug.

**Next:** decide the product-axis question, then rerun the 13 pilot units to confirm the fixes and run all 54 pages. Nothing is committed; say the word and I'll commit the skill and pilot separately.

## Claude

Done. The pilot README still describes problem 1 in terms of gaps; I'll reword it once you decide the column question.

The broader question, concretely: should a column mean "a surface the reader is sitting in" only? If yes, the two audience columns dissolve like this:

- **admin-console records** become `product: claude` (Team/Enterprise org settings live in claude.ai) or `claude-gov` (the Config page), with `actor: admin`. The M365 setup row then shows admin and end-user variants in the same cell, distinguished by actor in the appendix.
- **developer-platform records** become `product: claude` with `actor: developer`, since submission and the dashboard are claude.ai admin settings or Console. The T-051 to T-069 rows would then live in one column, which is honest but makes those rows nearly empty across the table.

My recommendation is yes to the first and a compromise on the second: keep `developer-platform` as a column because those tasks have no end-user counterpart, and a docs lead reading the row wants to know "who builds this" more than "which surface". Both changes are a taxonomy edit plus a rerun of merge and reconcile; the 71 extraction records don't change, so no re-extraction cost.

## User

yes, you are correct on both fronts, but i'd add the following nuance: `admin-console` is not a separate product, it is a surface within a product that `actor: admin` uses.  there's a similar issue in the taxonomy where `ui` and `admin-console` overlap: an admin console can be a UI, and an administrator can also use a CLI or configuration file.

In general, we should separate along these dimensions:

* product: which product or deployment does this task concern?
* actor: who performs it?
* surface: where do they perform it?
* mechanism: how do they perform it?
* outcome: what does completing it accomplish?


regrading `developer-platform`: what about T-041? that's a bit developer-related. instead of including it in the product inventory, let's put it into a separate view. let's keep the inventory focused on product-related tasks and create a new view for builder/publisher/developer related tasks, which will inventory shared development and publication workflows.

also, looks like the pilot is having trouble with task matching. the note on T-033 P3 is accurate: the page never documents an install step, so it cannot be a variant of "install a plugin". we should tighten matching around outcome: does this procedure *really* accomplish the task it belongs to?

maybe context isolation is going too far, and workers/reconcilers are left without the information they need. i don't think we should forbid source access, like, reconciliation needs targeted source reads otherwise it has no choice but to trust the info it was given.

## Claude

All three points land. Implementing: five-dimension schema with an outcome check, two views, and targeted source access for reconcilers. Then the pilot reruns since records need the new fields.

docs/1-audit/task-matrix; cat > .claude/skills/task-matrix/references/schema.md <<'EOF'\n# Data model\n\n## Five dimensions\n\nEvery procedure record is described along five independent dimensions.\nDo not collapse them into each other: an admin can use a CLI, a UI can be an\nadmin console, and the same surface hosts many mechanisms.\n\n| dimension   | question                                  | values                                   |\n| ----------- | ----------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------- |\n| `product`   | which product or deployment does this concern? | IDs in `taxonomy.yaml` → `products`  |\n| `actor`     | who performs it?                          | `end-user`, `admin`, `developer`         |\n| `surface`   | where do they perform it?                 | enum below                               |\n| `mechanism` | how do they perform it?                   | enum below                               |\n| `outcome`   | what does completing it accomplish?       | one clause, from the text                |\n\n### surface\n\n| surface             | meaning                                                             |\n| ------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------- |\n| `app-settings`      | the product's own settings / Customize area                         |\n| `admin-settings`    | an organization admin area (claude.ai admin settings, Gov Config page, Tag admin) |\n| `chat`              | inside a conversation with Claude                                   |\n| `terminal`          | a shell                                                             |\n| `filesystem`        | files on disk (SKILL.md, settings.json, plist, manifest, .zip)      |\n| `web-portal`        | a separate website: Console, submission portal, directory site      |\n| `third-party-admin` | another vendor's admin tool (Entra, GitHub settings, Slack admin)   |\n| `other`             | explain in `notes`                                                  |\n\n### mechanism\n\n| mechanism        | meaning                                                   |\n| ---------------- | --------------------------------------------------------- |\n| `click-through`  | navigate and click controls                                |\n| `command`        | run a command                                              |\n| `edit-file`      | create or edit a file                                      |\n| `api-call`       | HTTP or SDK call                                           |\n| `prompt-claude`  | ask Claude in natural language to do it                    |\n| `request-person` | ask another person (usually an admin) to do it             |\n| `other`          | explain in `notes`                                         |\n\n## Entities\n\n**Task** — a reader goal, imperative *verb + object* (\"install a plugin\").\nProduct-agnostic. Each task in `taxonomy.yaml` carries an `outcome`: the\nstate of the world when the task is done (\"the plugin is installed and its\ncomponents are available to the user in this product\"). Tasks also carry a\n`view`: `product` or `builder` (see Views).\n\n**Procedure family** — same task, same `surface` + `mechanism`. IDs per\ntask: `P1`, `P2`, ….\n\n**Variant** — same family, but steps, actor, or preconditions differ enough\nthat a reader following one would not succeed by following the other. IDs\n`P1a`, `P1b`, …. Different wording for the same steps is not a variant; it is\n`terminology-only` in the diff.\n\nSequential **stages** of one path (admin registers app → admin configures\nclient → user signs in) are ONE variant with the stages concatenated, not one\nvariant per stage. Split only when a reader would follow a different path.\n\nWorked example, \"install a plugin\":\n- `P1` app-settings / click-through — `P1a` Customize → Plugins → Browse →\n  Install (Cowork); `P1b` same but only the Organization tab is available\n  (Government). Same surface and mechanism, different precondition → variants.\n- `P2` app-settings / click-through, upload path — arguably the same family\n  as P1; the reconciler decides by asking whether a reader would treat\n  \"upload a .zip\" and \"browse the catalog\" as the same path. They would not,\n  so it is `P2`.\n- Admin allowlists the plugin in admin-settings → not this task at all; its\n  outcome is \"the plugin is available for members to install\", which is\n  T-040 \"add a plugin for an organization\".\n\n## The outcome rule (task matching)\n\nA procedure belongs to a task only if completing the procedure's steps\nachieves the task's `outcome`. Test it explicitly:\n\n1. Write the record's `outcome` from the text: what is true after the last step?\n2. Compare to the candidate task's `outcome` in the taxonomy.\n3. If the procedure only *points toward* the task (a link to a directory, a\n   \"see also\", a prerequisite) it does not belong to that task. Either it\n   belongs to a different task whose outcome it does meet, or it is `NEW`, or\n   it is not a procedure and should not be extracted.\n4. If the procedure is one stage of the task (admin half of a two-actor\n   setup) it does belong, with `stage_of` set to the task and `partial: true`.\n\nWrong: \"browse claude.com/plugins\" filed under \"install a plugin\". Nothing\nis installed after the last step.\n\n## Views\n\n`taxonomy.yaml` marks each task with a `view`:\n\n- **`product`** — the outcome lands inside a product for its users or admins.\n  Rendered as tasks × products in `inventory.md`.\n- **`builder`** — the outcome is an artifact or a listing: build, test,\n  package, submit, track review, update, delist. Rendered as lifecycle\n  `stage` × `artifact` (skill, plugin, connector, mcp-app) in\n  `builder-inventory.md`, so shared development and publication workflows\n  line up across artifact types. Builder tasks carry `artifact` and `stage`.\n\nThe same verb can appear in both views on different tasks: \"create a skill\nin Customize > Skills\" is product; \"author a skill package on disk\" is builder.\n\n## Source access\n\nWorkers extract only from their own unit but MAY open the other parts of the\nsame page (listed in the unit as `sibling_units`) to resolve a heading chain\nor a \"see above\" reference. Reconcilers work from records but MUST make\ntargeted reads of the source unit around each record's anchor when deciding\nwhether to merge two records, whether a record meets the task outcome, or\nwhen two records contradict. Record what you read in `checked`.\n\n## Evidence\n\nEvery record carries `anchor` (nearest heading above, verbatim) and `quote`\n(≤ 30 words copied exactly from inside the procedure). `validate.py`\nnormalizes whitespace and backticks and requires the quote to be a substring\nof the unit. No quote, no record.\n\n## What counts as a procedure\n\nAn ordered set of actions a reader performs to reach an outcome. Concept\ndescriptions, scope tables, limits, and FAQ answers with no actions are not\nprocedures. A single sentence \"Go to Settings > Connectors and click Add\" is.\nTroubleshooting entries are procedures for a \"troubleshoot X\" task only when\nthey give steps.\n\n## Extraction record\n\n```json\n{\n  \"task_id\": \"T-004\",\n  \"new_task\": null,\n  \"product\": \"claude-desktop\",\n  \"product_evidence\": null,\n  \"actor\": \"end-user\",\n  \"surface\": \"app-settings\",\n  \"mechanism\": \"click-through\",\n  \"outcome\": \"the remote MCP server is listed as a connector and can be enabled in chats\",\n  \"partial\": false,\n  \"stage_of\": null,\n  \"preconditions\": [\"Pro, Max, Team, or Enterprise plan\"],\n  \"steps\": [\"Open Settings > Connectors\", \"Click Add custom connector\", \"Paste the server URL\", \"Click Add\"],\n  \"evidence\": {\"anchor\": \"## Add a custom connector\", \"quote\": \"Click Add custom connector and paste the remote MCP server URL\"},\n  \"notes\": \"\"\n}\n```\n\n`new_task` is `{\"label\": \"verb object\", \"outcome\": \"…\", \"why\": \"…\"}` only when\nno taxonomy task's outcome is met; then `task_id` is `\"NEW\"`.\n`product_evidence` is required when `product` differs from the unit's default\nand must contain the product's name. `steps` ≤ 12, ordered, paraphrased, no\ninvented steps.\n\n## Per-task matrix record (reconciler output)\n\n```json\n{\n  \"task_id\": \"T-004\",\n  \"task\": \"add a custom connector\",\n  \"view\": \"product\",\n  \"families\": [\n    {\"family_id\": \"P1\", \"surface\": \"app-settings\", \"mechanism\": \"click-through\", \"label\": \"Add via Settings > Connectors\",\n     \"variants\": [\n       {\"variant_id\": \"P1a\", \"label\": \"Paste URL, OAuth in browser\", \"actor\": \"end-user\",\n        \"products\": [\"claude-web\", \"claude-desktop\"], \"preconditions\": [\"paid plan\"],\n        \"steps\": [\"…\"], \"sources\": [{\"unit_id\": \"connectors--custom--remote-mcp\", \"anchor\": \"## …\"}]}\n     ]}\n  ],\n  \"cells\": {\n    \"claude-desktop\": {\"status\": \"documented\", \"variants\": [\"P1a\"], \"diff\": [\"same\"], \"note\": \"\"},\n    \"claude-gov\":     {\"status\": \"documented\", \"variants\": [\"P1b\"], \"diff\": [\"precondition-differs\"], \"note\": \"admin must allowlist first\"},\n    \"cowork\":         {\"status\": \"absent\", \"variants\": [], \"diff\": [\"absent\"], \"note\": \"\"}\n  },\n  \"misfiled\": [{\"unit_id\": \"…\", \"anchor\": \"…\", \"reason\": \"outcome not met: nothing is installed after the last step\", \"suggest\": \"T-001 or NEW\"}],\n  \"checked\": [\"connectors--custom--remote-mcp ## Add a custom connector\"],\n  \"summary\": \"≤ 25 words.\",\n  \"open_questions\": []\n}\n```\n\nDiff types per cell, relative to the other documented cells for the task:\n`same`, `terminology-only`, `steps-differ`, `actor-differs`,\n`surface-differs`, `mechanism-differs`, `precondition-differs`, `absent`,\n`only-here`. A cell may carry several.\nEOF\necho ok",

}

If the same steps apply to two products and the page says so, emit two records.\n- **Outcome rule.** Before choosing `task_id`, write the record's `outcome` from the text. Pick the task whose taxonomy `outcome` is satisfied when the steps are done. A procedure that only points toward a task (a link, a \"see also\", a prerequisite) does not belong to it. If no task's outcome fits, use `\"NEW\"` with a label, outcome, and one-line reason. If it is one stage of a multi-actor task, keep the task and set `partial: true`, `stage_of` to the task ID.\n- `product` defaults to `product_default`. A UI label such as \"Customize > Skills\" is not product evidence.\n- `surface` and `mechanism` are separate. An admin clicking in admin settings is `admin-settings` + `click-through`; an admin editing a plist is `filesystem` + `edit-file`.\n- `evidence.quote`: ≤ 30 words copied verbatim from inside the procedure; it is grepped. `evidence.anchor`: the nearest heading line above, verbatim with its `#`s.\n- Steps ≤ 12, ordered, short imperatives, nothing the text does not state.\n- Do not extract concepts, tables, limits, or FAQ answers with no actions.\n- If the unit has no procedures, set `no_procedures: true` and `records: []`.\n\nOutput only the JSON file. Reply with at most two sentences.\nEOF\ncat > references/reconcile-prompt.md <<'EOF'\n# Reconciler prompt (phases 4–5, one agent per task)\n\nFill in `{TASK_PATH}`, `{UNITS_DIR}`, and `{OUT_PATH}`.\n\n---\n\nYou are reconciling every extracted procedure record for one task into a matrix record, and checking that each record really belongs to the task.\n\nRead, in this order:\n1. `{SKILL_DIR}/references/schema.md` — dimensions, family/variant rule, outcome rule, diff types.\n2. `{SKILL_DIR}/references/taxonomy.yaml` — this task's `outcome` and `view`, the product list, and neighbouring tasks a misfiled record might belong to.\n3. `{TASK_PATH}` — JSON with `task_id`, `task`, `outcome`, `view`, `products_in_scope`, and `records` (each with `_unit_id`, `_page`, and evidence).\n\nSource access: the units are in `{UNITS_DIR}/<unit_id>.json` (field `text`). Make targeted reads around each record's `anchor` whenever you decide to merge two records, split them, or judge whether a record meets the task outcome. Do not read whole pages you have no record from. List what you read in `checked`.\n\nWrite `{OUT_PATH}` as the per-task matrix record in schema.md.\n\nRules:\n- **Outcome check first.** For each record, ask: after its last step, is the task's `outcome` true? If not, move the record to `misfiled` with the reason and a suggested task ID (or NEW) and exclude it from families and cells. A `partial: true` record for a multi-actor task passes if it is a genuine stage of the path.\n- Families = same `surface` + `mechanism`. Within a family, sequential stages of one path become ONE variant with the stages concatenated (summarize a stage as one step if the total would exceed 12). Split into variants only when a reader would follow a different path or a different gate.\n- IDs: `P1`, `P2`, … by number of products using the family, then `P1a`, `P1b`, ….\n- Keep every source on each variant.\n- Fill a cell for every product in `products_in_scope`. No records → `absent`. If only one product documents the task, its cell is `only-here`.\n- Diff types are relative to the other documented cells. Use `terminology-only` for name-only differences; do not create a variant for that.\n- `summary`: ONE sentence, ≤ 25 words; it is a table cell.\n- `open_questions`: things the records and targeted reads could not settle.\n\nOutput only the JSON file. Reply with at most two sentences.\nEOF\npython3 - <<'EOF'\nimport pathlib,re\n# split.py: sibling_units\np=pathlib.Path('scripts/split.py'); s=p.read_text()\ns=s.replace('''        for i, (chain, body) in enumerate(chunks):\n            suffix = f\"--part{i+1:02d}\" if len(chunks) > 1 else \"\"\n            uid = unit_id(p[\"path\"], suffix)\n            unit = {\"unit_id\": uid, \"page\": p[\"path\"], \"url\": p[\"url\"],\n                    \"product_default\": product_for(p[\"path\"]),\n                    \"part\": i + 1, \"parts\": len(chunks),''','''        uids = [unit_id(p[\"path\"], f\"--part{i+1:02d}\" if len(chunks) > 1 else \"\") for i in range(len(chunks))]\n        for i, (chain, body) in enumerate(chunks):\n            uid = uids[i]\n            unit = {\"unit_id\": uid, \"page\": p[\"path\"], \"url\": p[\"url\"],\n                    \"product_default\": product_for(p[\"path\"]),\n                    \"part\": i + 1, \"parts\": len(chunks),\n                    \"sibling_units\": [u for u in uids if u != uid],''')\ns=s.replace('    (\"connectors/building/\", \"developer-platform\"),\\n','    (\"connectors/building/\", \"claude\"),\\n')\np.write_text(s)\n# validate.py: new enums + fields\np=pathlib.Path('scripts/validate.py'); s=p.read_text()\ns=s.replace('MECH = {\"ui\", \"cli\", \"config-file\", \"admin-console\", \"api\", \"request-to-admin\", \"other\"}',\n'MECH = {\"click-through\", \"command\", \"edit-file\", \"api-call\", \"prompt-claude\", \"request-person\", \"other\"}\\nSURF = {\"app-settings\", \"admin-settings\", \"chat\", \"terminal\", \"filesystem\", \"web-portal\", \"third-party-admin\", \"other\"}')\ns=s.replace('''    if r.get(\"mechanism\") not in MECH: errs.append(f\"bad mechanism {r.get('mechanism')}\")''',\n'''    if r.get(\"mechanism\") not in MECH: errs.append(f\"bad mechanism {r.get('mechanism')}\")\n    if r.get(\"surface\") not in SURF: errs.append(f\"bad surface {r.get('surface')}\")\n    if not (r.get(\"outcome\") or \"\").strip(): errs.append(\"no outcome\")\n    if r.get(\"partial\") and r.get(\"stage_of\") not in tasks: errs.append(\"partial without valid stage_of\")''')\ns=s.replace('''        if not nt.get(\"label\") or not nt.get(\"why\"): errs.append(\"NEW without label/why\")''','''        if not nt.get(\"label\") or not nt.get(\"why\") or not nt.get(\"outcome\"): errs.append(\"NEW without label/outcome/why\")''')\np.write_text(s)\n# merge.py: pass outcome + view\np=pathlib.Path('scripts/merge.py'); s=p.read_text()\ns=s.replace('''        json.dump({\"task_id\": tid, \"task\": tasks[tid].get(\"label\", tid), \"definition\": tasks[tid].get(\"definition\", \"\"),''',\n'''        json.dump({\"task_id\": tid, \"task\": tasks[tid].get(\"label\", tid), \"definition\": tasks[tid].get(\"definition\", \"\"),\n                   \"outcome\": tasks[tid].get(\"outcome\", \"\"), \"view\": tasks[tid].get(\"view\", \"product\"),\n                   \"artifact\": tasks[tid].get(\"artifact\", \"\"), \"stage\": tasks[tid].get(\"stage\", \"\"),''')\np.write_text(s)\nEOF\necho ok",
  "description": "Rewrite worker/reconciler prompts and update split, validate, merge for the new schema"
}

<details><summary>Tool result</summary>

````
---
name: task-matrix
description: Build a task–procedure–difference matrix from the docs corpus in corpus/. Use when the user asks to inventory documented tasks, compare how a task's procedure differs across products, run the extraction pipeline or its pilot, or regenerate docs/1-audit/inventory.md. Orchestrates parallel Opus extraction workers, deterministic validation, per-task reconciliation, and script-rendered output.
---

# task-matrix

Produces two inventories. The **product view** is tasks × products: each cell
says which procedure variant(s) a product documents for that task and how
they differ. The **builder view** is lifecycle stage × artifact type for
development and publication tasks. Every record is described on five
dimensions: product, actor, surface, mechanism, outcome.
Models emit JSON records; scripts validate, merge, and render. No model ever
writes the final table.

Data model, vocabulary rules, and worker contracts live in `references/`.
Read `references/schema.md` before doing anything else.

## Layout

```
.claude/skills/task-matrix/
  references/schema.md              data model + family/variant rule (read first)
  references/taxonomy.yaml          frozen task + product vocabulary with IDs
  references/extraction-prompt.md   worker contract (phase 2)
````

</details>

Written so a worker can test \"after the procedure's last step, is this true?\". Make outcomes distinct enough that neighbouring tasks (T-019 setup vs T-023 tool policies; T-042 enable vs T-045 test) do not both accept the same procedure.\n- `view`: \"product\" when the outcome lands inside a product for its users or admins (installing, enabling, configuring, using, managing, troubleshooting, admin distribution/allowlisting). \"builder\" when the outcome is an artifact or a listing (authoring a package on disk, validating/testing an artifact before publication, packaging, submitting to a directory, tracking review, updating a listing, delisting).\n- Builder tasks: `artifact` is one of skill, plugin, connector, mcp-app. `stage` is one of build, test, package, submit, review, distribute, maintain, remove.\n- Where one current task straddles both views (e.g. T-043 \"create a skill\": end users create one in Customize > Skills, developers author SKILL.md on disk), split it into two tasks with different IDs and outcomes. Append new IDs after T-069; do not renumber existing ones.   "prompt": "You are reconciling every extracted procedure record for one task into a matrix record, and checking that each record really belongs to the task. This task has 13 records from two pages documenting the same connector for different deployments; expect multi-stage admin+user paths. Merge sequential stages of one path into one variant.\n\nRead, in this order:\n1. ~/personal/anthropic-take-home/.claude/skills/task-matrix/references/schema.md — dimensions, family/variant rule, outcome rule, diff types, and the per-task matrix record format.\n2. ~/personal/anthropic-take-home/.claude/skills/task-matrix/references/taxonomy.yaml — this task's outcome and view, the product list, and neighbouring tasks a misfiled record might belong to (T-023 in particular).\n3. ~/personal/anthropic-take-home/docs/1-audit/task-matrix/pilot/tasks/T-019.json — task_id, task, outcome, view, products_in_scope, records (each with _unit_id, _page, evidence).\n\nSource access: units are in ~/personal/anthropic-take-home/docs/1-audit/task-matrix/pilot/units/<unit_id>.json (field `text`). Make targeted reads around each record's anchor whenever you decide to merge two records, split them, or judge whether a record meets the task outcome. Do not read whole pages you have no record from. List what you read in `checked`.\n\nWrite ~/personal/anthropic-take-home/docs/1-audit/task-matrix/pilot/matrix/T-019.json as the per-task matrix record (keys: task_id, task, view, families, cells, misfiled, checked, summary, open_questions).\n\nRules:\n- Outcome check first. For each record: after its last step, is the task's outcome true? If not, move it to `misfiled` with the reason and a suggested task ID (or NEW) and exclude it from families and cells. A partial: true record for a multi-actor task passes if it is a genuine stage of the path.\n- Families = same surface + mechanism of the path's defining step; a multi-surface path (Entra registration → Desktop config → user sign-in) is ONE variant whose family is set by the stage the reader must choose between alternatives on (e.g. in-app config vs managed config file). Summarize stages as single steps if the total would exceed 12.\n- IDs: P1, P2, … by number of products using the family, then P1a, P1b, ….\n- Keep every source on each variant (unit_id + anchor).\n- Fill a cell for every product in products_in_scope. No records → absent. If only one product documents the task, its cell is only-here.\n- Diff types are relative to the other documented cells. terminology-only for name-only differences; no variant for that.\n- summary: ONE sentence, ≤ 25 words.\n- open_questions: what records and targeted reads could not settle.\n\nOutput only the JSON file. Reply with at most two sentences."
}
```

Cell for every product in products_in_scope: the documented product gets status \"documented\", its variant, diff [\"only-here\"]; every other product gets status \"absent\", variants [], diff [\"absent\"], note \"\". `checked` lists the unit + anchor you read. summary: one sentence ≤ 25 words. open_questions: [] unless the record is ambiguous.\n\nWrite all 15 files. Reply with the list of task IDs written and any you marked misfiled, nothing else."
}

## Claude

Pilot v2 is complete and rendered. Everything is on disk, uncommitted.

**What changed in the skill** ([schema.md](.claude/skills/task-matrix/references/schema.md)):
- Five independent dimensions: product, actor, surface, mechanism, outcome. `admin-console` is gone as a product and as a mechanism; it is now `admin-settings` surface with `actor: admin`.
- Task matching is by outcome. Every taxonomy task carries an outcome clause, workers must write the record's outcome before choosing a task, and reconcilers re-check it against source and move failures to a `misfiled` list.
- Two views. Product view is tasks × products. Builder view is lifecycle stage × artifact type, so T-041 and its connector counterpart T-058 now sit on the same "submit" row.
- Reconcilers make targeted source reads around each record's anchor. 90 reads across 33 tasks in the pilot.
- Gap analysis removed.

**Pilot v2 results** ([pilot/README.md](docs/1-audit/task-matrix/pilot/README.md), outputs in [pilot/out/](docs/1-audit/task-matrix/pilot/out/inventory.md)):

| | v1 | v2 |
|---|---|---|
| records | 71, 0 NEW | 79, 6 NEW |
| M365 setup task | 6 families / 9 variants | 2 families / 6 variants |
| misfiled by reconcilers | n/a | 2 |
| max summary length | 64 words | 25 words |

**What the outcome rule caught:** the plugins overview now files as "browse the plugin directory" instead of "install a plugin". Enterprise role delegation became NEW rather than a submit variant. A reconciler pulled a tenant-level Entra consent revocation out of "manage installed connectors" after reading the source. All six NEW candidates look like real tasks and are listed in the README for adding before the full run.

**Still open, for your call:**
- `claude-web` and `claude-desktop` appear as near-empty columns beside the undistinguished `claude` column because the docs almost never name the surface. Either merge them into one column for this corpus or keep them and let the audit memo say so.
- Cross-task overlaps (M365 setup vs tool policies) are now flagged with evidence in `open_questions` but nothing resolves them. A pass that reads only those lists across tasks is the next thing to build.

## User

let's merge them into one column for this corpus, we can consider them to be the same product on different surfaces.

## Claude

Done. The product view now has five columns: Claude (claude.ai and Desktop), Claude Desktop on third-party inference, Claude for Government, Claude Tag, and Cowork.

What changed:
- The taxonomy drops `claude-web` and `claude-desktop`. The `claude` entry is relabeled "Claude (claude.ai and Claude Desktop)" and its name list accepts "claude.ai", "web app", and "desktop app" as evidence.
- `schema.md` says the client goes in a record's `notes` when a page names it, and is not modeled as a product or surface.
- The three affected pilot records were remapped and their two tasks (T-041 plugin submission, T-058 connector submission) re-reconciled with source reads. Both now sit in one `claude` cell with their variants split by surface: admin-settings form vs Console portal for plugins, org portal vs MCPB form for connectors.
- The pilot README records the decision and the updated recommendation: add the six NEW tasks, then run all 54 pages, with the cross-task pass built before the final render.

Still uncommitted. The skill and the two pilot directories are ready to commit whenever you want them in.

## User

cowork, claude-web, and claude-desktop should also be merged, since cowork is a mode inside claude-web and claude-desktop.

## Claude

Done. The product view is now four columns: Claude (claude.ai, Desktop, and Cowork mode), Claude Desktop on third-party inference, Claude for Government, and Claude Tag.

Changes:
- `cowork` removed from the taxonomy; "cowork" added to the `claude` name list so overrides on that word still validate. The split script now defaults `cowork/` pages to `claude`.
- Seven Cowork records remapped with "mode: cowork" in their notes. Five tasks re-reconciled with source reads.
- One reconciler reversed an override on its own: the Government skills page says packaging can be done in a Cowork task, and a worker had moved that record to `claude`. The reconciler read the surrounding text, found it scoped to the Government deployment, and put it back under `claude-gov` with the call flagged in open questions. That is the source-access change doing what you wanted.

The plugin rows now read as intended: installing, adding a marketplace, configuring components, and removing a plugin each compare Claude against Government in one row, with the Cowork-mode path labeled inside the variant rather than occupying its own column.

Pilot README reflects the merge. Still uncommitted.

## Claude

From the pilot's extraction and reconcile passes:

| candidate | source page | why no existing task fit | proposed view |
|---|---|---|---|
| delegate directory management access | connectors/building/submission | An Enterprise owner grants a member a custom role so they can submit; nothing is filed after the last step, so it is a precondition of "submit a connector", not the task itself | builder / connector / submit |
| block a connector's individual tools for yourself | connectors/custom/remote-mcp | T-023 is an admin restricting members and T-008 is the in-chat permission card; neither covers an end user setting a tool to Blocked in their own Customize > Connectors | product |
| troubleshoot missing or incomplete connector search results | connectors/microsoft/365 (two records) | T-015 covers tools failing to appear or invoke; here the tools work and the content search returns empty or partial results | product |
| troubleshoot a connector that does not appear in the product | third-party m365, part 4 | T-013, T-014, and T-015 all presuppose a connector that is present; these rows cover version and config-parsing failures before any connection is attempted | product |
| check plugin submission review status | plugins/submit | T-041's outcome is met once the form is filed; T-059 (submissions dashboard) is connector-scoped | builder / plugin / review |
| validate a skill package before upload | skills/how-to | Came from a reconciler misfile: the "Before uploading" procedure runs `skills-ref validate` and reviews the source but never gives Claude a matching task, so T-045's outcome ("confirmed the skill triggers") is not met | builder / skill / test |

The first five came from extraction workers using the NEW escape hatch. The sixth came from the single-record reconciler doing the outcome check against source. All six look like real tasks to me and are listed in the pilot README for adding to the taxonomy before the full run.

## Claude

All three fixed, and a dry run of the splitter over the full 54-page corpus produces 62 units with every product default valid against the taxonomy.

What was stale and is now fixed:
- **`split.py`** defaulted the four MCP tunnels pages to `claude-desktop`. Now `claude`. This would have rejected every record from those pages on the full run.
- **`schema.md`** examples and the extraction prompt used removed IDs and cited "in Cowork" as product evidence. Examples now use `claude` and `claude-tag`; the prompt says Cowork is a mode of `claude`, not a product.
- **28 pilot matrix files** carried `absent` cells for `claude-web`, `claude-desktop`, and `cowork`. Stripped, with `only-here` recomputed. Re-rendered output is unchanged, which confirms the renderer was already ignoring them.

Checked and clean: SKILL.md, the reconcile prompt, the taxonomy task definitions, and the validator and merge scripts carry no references to the removed products.

One judgment call I did not change: `claude-desktop-3p` (Claude Desktop on third-party inference) is still its own column. By the same logic you applied to Cowork it could be argued to be `claude` on a different deployment, but its procedures differ at every stage (Entra registration, managed config, brokered sign-in), so I've left it separate. Say the word if you want it folded too.
